## 2026-10-29T00:15:00Z — large-curve and prime-bit proof extension
### What was done (commands, files, features)
- Completed the large-curve native extension on the existing PR branch. Exact arbitrary-precision replay accepts public degree-five DAGs for secp256k1, secp256r1, secp384r1, and secp521r1, with 413, 415, 415, and 415 nodes. All curve/input witnesses recheck. A separate OpenSSL 3.5.7 helper matches all112 public point coordinates and all four parameter sets.
- The fresh mandatory `cargo test --release --lib --locked -- --test-threads=4` completed on merged revision `a4adc94d`: 5,017 passed, zero failed,122 ignored,1,132.47 seconds. The earlier interrupted run was preserved; the detached complete rerun is recorded in `extensions/large_curves/validation/library-tests.json`. Native crate tests passed43 cases; formatting and strict Clippy passed.
- Added the independently audited F_p-coefficient bit-restricted upper theorem: normalized lookup inputs give `d_F<=3*ell+2`, raw inputs give `d_F<=max(3*ell+2,d_raw)`, with rational-image and surjective auxiliary-encoding hypotheses. Arbitrary multilinear lookups have `d_raw<=4*ell`; affine lookups retain the normalized cap. This is not a GF(2) carry-circuit theorem.
- Added eight actual checked Lean dependencies in `PrimeBitLocal.lean`, including quadratic cancellation and nonzero-discriminant/diagonal consequences. Default `lake build` passed770 jobs, and fresh kernel replay of the new module exited zero. The new source SHA is `d93729a96a0e92c5ad381980ff6ed0d0ba1219f14e13beb24e9addb61a7bfb01`. The old twelve-lemma source and its four ProofRefs remain byte-identical.
- Updated the exact proof map, source notes, requirement status, coverage diagram, reproduction commands, and four-page report PDF. Visually inspected every final page. Preserved exact fixed-fixture inputs, public point witnesses, upper DAGs, construction and independent replay records, OpenSSL comparisons, and development receipts.
### Decisions made (and why)
- Distinguished mathematical first/lower degree proofs from numerical upper-DAG replay. No new large-field degree-four closure or rank computation is claimed.
- Kept coefficient-field cardinality, F_p-coefficient bit restrictions, characteristic-two Boolean descent, and GF(2) carry circuits as separate presentations. Their bounds do not transfer by relabelling variables, and lookup/proof size may be exponential.
- Kept the complete filtered-closure bounds and curve-provenance/group arguments outside the Lean claim. The new local lemmas expose their exact ring/field hypotheses and use only standard foundations.
- Used local Conductor T-5 for final validation/publication after T-4's lease expired; its scopes include the study and both dated logs. No shared-host reservation is asserted.
### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)
- Push the completed extension to PR #2795 and follow its exact-head validation, size, artifacts and required review gates. Large binary numerical certificates, growing lower bounds/exact degrees, E12 replay/encoding audits, and full Lean closure formalization remain open.
### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)
- Workspace restart killed a foreground library run before its result. Saved the interrupted output, relaunched the complete command detached from the tool session, and obtained the clean full-suite result above.
- The restart had removed the temporary Lean compiler. Restored the exact official4.34.1 toolchain, verified its version/commit, reused the pinned Mathlib cache, and checked the actual new source. No uncompiled theorem is counted as checked.

## 2026-10-29 02:22Z - XP-20261016 prime-base oracle tuning: run, validation, results PR
- Froze the protocol and moved the card to `ready` in PR #2810 (blob `0ee2e287…`, code.commit `0e124fb1b`). Resume checkpoint pushed to S3, and `checkpoint.sh gate` returned 0.
- The experiment director wrote frozen card `CARD-XP-20261016-prime-base-oracle-tuning-01` and the mechanical executor launched it, binding the binary and every spec by hash. Run: `run.sh all research/prime_base_oracle_tuning_20261029` (01:29:21Z–01:56:09Z, one pinned core).
  - All 22 sessions verified, and every audit came back ok.
  - `run.sh verify` replayed every session identically.
  - Relay cost: director $0.38, executor $0.006 (`tools/token_usage.py`).
- Results, measured on the holdout, 96 pairs:
  - 20 bits: N 32 → 45 with the same oracle gives Σ S_sel/Σ S_inc 0.818 [0.773, 0.866], 0.798 [0.752, 0.847] and 0.726 [0.677, 0.778] at w = 0, 0.1 and 1, so §7.3 reads it as an improvement.
  - 24 and 28 bits: the smaller N falls at w = 0 and rises at w = 1 (1.126 and 1.193), which the protocol reads as relabelling.
  - 32 bits: the selection is the incumbent.
- An independent validator subagent passed the 20-bit row: holdout-P20 replayed 672/672, the 84 comparisons were re-run byte-identically, and the ratios were recomputed independently. `validation_gate.py evidence` reports valid for the study and for `benchmarks/20261029-prime-fb-size-20bit/`.
- Added the README, run report, figures and PDFs (research-visuals), and a benchmarks entry and row.

## 2026-10-29T00:15:00Z — large-curve and prime-bit proof extension
### What was done (commands, files, features)
- Completed the large-curve native extension on the existing PR branch. Exact arbitrary-precision replay accepts public degree-five DAGs for secp256k1, secp256r1, secp384r1, and secp521r1, with 413, 415, 415, and 415 nodes. All curve/input witnesses recheck. A separate OpenSSL 3.5.7 helper matches all112 public point coordinates and all four parameter sets.
- The fresh mandatory `cargo test --release --lib --locked -- --test-threads=4` completed on merged revision `a4adc94d`: 5,017 passed, zero failed,122 ignored,1,132.47 seconds. The earlier interrupted run was preserved; the detached complete rerun is recorded in `extensions/large_curves/validation/library-tests.json`. Native crate tests passed43 cases; formatting and strict Clippy passed.
- Added the independently audited F_p-coefficient bit-restricted upper theorem: normalized lookup inputs give `d_F<=3*ell+2`, raw inputs give `d_F<=max(3*ell+2,d_raw)`, with rational-image and surjective auxiliary-encoding hypotheses. Arbitrary multilinear lookups have `d_raw<=4*ell`; affine lookups retain the normalized cap. This is not a GF(2) carry-circuit theorem.
- Added eight actual checked Lean dependencies in `PrimeBitLocal.lean`, including quadratic cancellation and nonzero-discriminant/diagonal consequences. Default `lake build` passed770 jobs, and fresh kernel replay of the new module exited zero. The new source SHA is `d93729a96a0e92c5ad381980ff6ed0d0ba1219f14e13beb24e9addb61a7bfb01`. The old twelve-lemma source and its four ProofRefs remain byte-identical.
- Updated the exact proof map, source notes, requirement status, coverage diagram, reproduction commands, and four-page report PDF. Visually inspected every final page. Preserved exact fixed-fixture inputs, public point witnesses, upper DAGs, construction and independent replay records, OpenSSL comparisons, and development receipts.
### Decisions made (and why)
- Distinguished mathematical first/lower degree proofs from numerical upper-DAG replay. No new large-field degree-four closure or rank computation is claimed.
- Kept coefficient-field cardinality, F_p-coefficient bit restrictions, characteristic-two Boolean descent, and GF(2) carry circuits as separate presentations. Their bounds do not transfer by relabelling variables, and lookup/proof size may be exponential.
- Kept the complete filtered-closure bounds and curve-provenance/group arguments outside the Lean claim. The new local lemmas expose their exact ring/field hypotheses and use only standard foundations.
- Used local Conductor T-5 for final validation/publication after T-4's lease expired; its scopes include the study and both dated logs. No shared-host reservation is asserted.
### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)
- Push the completed extension to PR #2795 and follow its exact-head validation, size, artifacts and required review gates. Large binary numerical certificates, growing lower bounds/exact degrees, E12 replay/encoding audits, and full Lean closure formalization remain open.
### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

- The restart had removed the temporary Lean compiler. Restored the exact official4.34.1 toolchain, verified its version/commit, reused the pinned Mathlib cache, and checked the actual new source. No uncompiled theorem is counted as checked.

## 06:05Z — opt-in `stride-renew-deferred-guard` target source (Claude)

### What was done (commands, files, features)
- `src/cryptanalysis/ic_boundary.rs`: new `TargetSource::StrideRenewDeferredGuard` and `TargetSource::renews_stride()`. It is `StrideRenew` with the repeat guard moved after the oracle, so only targets that decompose are probed and inserted into `seen`. Every `== StrideRenew` check now calls `renews_stride()`, and the generator-coupling test sweep covers the new source.
- `src/cryptanalysis/ic_framework/{stages,mod}.rs`: `targets = stride-renew-deferred-guard` parses and maps to the new source. The stride test in `ic_framework/mod.rs` checks that both versions produce the same targets tried, the same relations, steps, restarts and renewals, the same answer, and the same doublings and scalar multiplications. It also checks that lookups equal the eager lookups plus the eager guard's skips, that additions rise by at most one per such skip, and that there are strictly fewer guard probes.
- Measured basis (P20 srenew record, XP-20261029 stride study): the relations phase recorded `trials` 3968, `target_guard_probes` 3968, `lookups` 3957, `repeated_targets_skipped` 11 and `relations` 31. The eager guard probes on every trial. The deferred one would probe about 31 times plus repeats.

### Decisions made (and why)
- **Opt-in variant; the default is unchanged.** All existing specs and records stay byte-reproducible.
- **Why it is real work removed, not relabelling:** it deletes one hash-map insert per trial from the loop. The target stream and rows are unchanged, because a repeat that does not decompose contributes nothing whether or not it is probed.
- **Expected effect, not measured:**
  - On its own the change is a w = 0 tie (the charged work is identical except for at most one addition per eager-skipped repeat), with a native saving at w > 0. Under the frozen rule a lone run would therefore read "unresolved".
  - The study that would follow pairs it with a smaller N at 28/32 bits, where the w = 0 optimum sits below the incumbent N. That pairing still has to be preregistered.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)
- CI must compile this and run the tests. No local build was possible: the disk has about 430 MB free.
- After it merges: freeze a protocol (deferred guard + N against the srenew incumbent, §5a band), then run the commit-image route via `isolab-experiment.yml`.
- Conductor is not installed in this container (`conductor` is not on PATH), so no reservation was taken.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)
- A `git grep` over the whole of `origin/main` timed out in the foreground and finished in the background. It found no further match sites.

### What was done (commands, files, features)

Wrote a prototype in `research/btn_ecdl_20261029/` of the paper's relation-finding step on Koblitz curve y^2+xy=x^3+1: constraint network for A1+A2=R with hashed factor bases, bucket-elimination contractor (stand-in for the unreviewed OpenAI contraction), Lemma-1 bitwise sampling. Verified (a) network count equals brute-force count of decompositions on GF(2^5) for 3 random R (run `test_enc.py 5`); (b) the same network for ECC2K-130 (GF(2^131), x^131+x^13+x^2+x+1): 105,062 variables, 104,801 constraints, 524,008 BTN edges after equality-tree conversion, max predicate arity 5; a witness from reference arithmetic on random curve points satisfies all predicates, and a one-bit flip violates one (`ecc2k130_check.py`, `ecc2k130_check.json`). End-to-end toy DLP on GF(2^5), r=11: see README once finished.

### Decisions made
No ECC2K-130 discrete logarithm was or can be computed: contraction needs the unreviewed algorithm and is exponential here. The 131-bit work is an encoding-validity and size check only. Bit-level multiplier chain uses O(n^2) edges, not the paper's O(n) Fourier construction (not implemented; binary fields need a characteristic-2 variant). Local checks only; no cloud experiment.

### Open threads
Conductor not installed in this session (no `.conductor/`), so no task was attached. n=7 toy needs treewidth ~34, beyond the dense contractor. No leaderboard row, no benchmark entry.

### Errors
First contractor attempts ran out of memory (einsum optimize, min-degree order); switched to min-fill ordering and n=5. 131-bit nonzero test initially had arity 131; replaced by an OR chain (Lemma 2).

## 06:05Z — opt-in `stride-renew-deferred-guard` target source (Claude)

### What was done (commands, files, features)
- `src/cryptanalysis/ic_boundary.rs`: new `TargetSource::StrideRenewDeferredGuard` and `TargetSource::renews_stride()`. It is `StrideRenew` with the repeat guard moved after the oracle, so only targets that decompose are probed and inserted into `seen`. Every `== StrideRenew` check now calls `renews_stride()`, and the generator-coupling test sweep covers the new source.
- `src/cryptanalysis/ic_framework/{stages,mod}.rs`: `targets = stride-renew-deferred-guard` parses and maps to the new source. The stride test in `ic_framework/mod.rs` checks that both versions produce the same targets tried, the same relations, steps, restarts and renewals, the same answer, and the same doublings and scalar multiplications. It also checks that lookups equal the eager lookups plus the eager guard's skips, that additions rise by at most one per such skip, and that there are strictly fewer guard probes.
- Measured basis (P20 srenew record, XP-20261029 stride study): the relations phase recorded `trials` 3968, `target_guard_probes` 3968, `lookups` 3957, `repeated_targets_skipped` 11 and `relations` 31. The eager guard probes on every trial. The deferred one would probe about 31 times plus repeats.

### Decisions made (and why)
- **Opt-in variant; the default is unchanged.** All existing specs and records stay byte-reproducible.
- **Why it is real work removed, not relabelling:** it deletes one hash-map insert per trial from the loop. The target stream and rows are unchanged, because a repeat that does not decompose contributes nothing whether or not it is probed.
- **Expected effect, not measured:**
  - On its own the change is a w = 0 tie (the charged work is identical except for at most one addition per eager-skipped repeat), with a native saving at w > 0. Under the frozen rule a lone run would therefore read "unresolved".
  - The study that would follow pairs it with a smaller N at 28/32 bits, where the w = 0 optimum sits below the incumbent N. That pairing still has to be preregistered.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)
- CI must compile this and run the tests. No local build was possible: the disk has about 430 MB free.
- After it merges: freeze a protocol (deferred guard + N against the srenew incumbent, §5a band), then run the commit-image route via `isolab-experiment.yml`.
- Conductor is not installed in this container (`conductor` is not on PATH), so no reservation was taken.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

## 2026-10-29T15:14:00Z — complete exact-pair theorem builds

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- Built the complete `GuardedExactPair.exact_pair_prime` theorem for every prime p>3, with the original distinct abscissas, nonsingular short-Weierstrass equation, affine rational target, rational base lifts and polynomial-system UNSAT hypotheses. The default pinned Lake build passed1,690 jobs. Its printed transitive axioms are exactly the standard foundations `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`.
- The proof constructs the degree-five refutation through explicit cubic rotations, interpolation inverses, staged resultant extraction and scalar-grid inversion. Rational lifts split the first specialized quadratic directly, including repeated roots/two-torsion. Polynomial UNSAT supplies the scalar nonvanishing condition; no unit ideal or bounded refutation is assumed. The unit ideal follows from the constructed derivation, and the earlier uniform degree-four obstruction gives exact last fall five.
- Defined the homogeneous-top relation space and its degree-specific classical Koszul combinations using the actual degrees/leading components of all six inputs. Proved exact first fall four, with a non-Koszul witness whose lift is nonzero and has degree at most three. The exact degree vector `(3,3,3,4,4,2)` and top forms are proved rather than input as hypotheses.
- Started the unchanged repository transitive axiom auditor on every new module and individual fresh kernel replays of all thirteen new modules. These checks are still running; the build result is not being relabelled as their completion.
- The first mandatory Rust validation attempt failed during compilation because five tracked prime20 fixture files were not materialized. Preserved that failed output/status, reserved the exact fixture directory with Conductor T-10, restored its HEAD bytes without reapplying the broader sparse profile, and restarted the full library command. No Rust code, fixture bytes or validation gate was changed.

### Decisions made (and why)

- Use the explicit scalar quadratic product identity instead of requiring an unnecessary Mathlib resultant/common-root-over-algebraic-closure bridge. Use actual polynomial derivations instead of a quotient assumption. These alternative proofs discharge the earlier central obligations without weakening the hypotheses or changing the generating presentation.
- Expose a stronger general field theorem with `6 != 0` as well as the original nonsingular/rational-target wrapper and explicit ZMod prime-field specialization. The stronger theorem does not need nonsingularity or a target ordinate; the original wrapper still includes them.
- Keep the first-fall convention explicit. This is the ordinary homogeneous-top/Koszul definition, not an implicit Boolean reduction or operational Weil-descent convention.
- Switched from the expiring local T-9 lease to T-11 for final proof checking/publication, and claimed T-12 for coverage documentation. The local coordination service has a four-attempt short-lease limit; no global reservation is asserted.

## 2026-10-29 — BTN relation-finding prototype (research/btn_ecdl_20261029/)

### What was done
Cloned `bochum-crypto/subexponential-ecdl` (read-only): it holds only the PDF, no code. Wrote a prototype in `research/btn_ecdl_20261029/` of the paper's relation-finding step on Koblitz curve y^2+xy=x^3+1: constraint network for A1+A2=R with hashed factor bases, bucket-elimination contractor (stand-in for the unreviewed OpenAI contraction), Lemma-1 bitwise sampling. Verified (a) network count equals brute-force count of decompositions on GF(2^5) for 3 random R (run `test_enc.py 5`); (b) the same network for ECC2K-130 (GF(2^131), x^131+x^13+x^2+x+1): 105,062 variables, 104,801 constraints, 524,008 BTN edges after equality-tree conversion, max predicate arity 5; a witness from reference arithmetic on random curve points satisfies all predicates, and a one-bit flip violates one (`ecc2k130_check.py`, `ecc2k130_check.json`). End-to-end toy DLP on GF(2^5), r=11: see README once finished.

### Decisions made
No ECC2K-130 discrete logarithm was or can be computed: contraction needs the unreviewed algorithm and is exponential here. The 131-bit work is an encoding-validity and size check only. Bit-level multiplier chain uses O(n^2) edges, not the paper's O(n) Fourier construction (not implemented; binary fields need a characteristic-2 variant). Local checks only; no cloud experiment.

### Open threads
Conductor not installed in this session (no `.conductor/`), so no task was attached. n=7 toy needs treewidth ~34, beyond the dense contractor. No leaderboard row, no benchmark entry.

### Errors
First contractor attempts ran out of memory (einsum optimize, min-degree order); switched to min-fill ordering and n=5. 131-bit nonzero test initially had arity 131; replaced by an OR chain (Lemma 2).

## 2026-10-29 — BTN index calculus over prime fields (research/btn_ecdl_prime_20261029/)

### What was done
Built the paper's prime-field ingredients: point validity and affine addition on y^2=x^3+ax+b over F_p as BTN predicates (schoolbook mod-p multiply, linear mod-p, range checks). Step 1, all vs brute force: mod-p multiply at p=11, 13 (6 products each, free count p^2); relation counts p=5 (6=6), 7 (10=10), 13 (16=16); first contraction p=5, 7, 11, 13 (4, 10, 8, 16). Step 2, end-to-end index calculus (m=2, hashed bases, Lemma-1 sampling, kernel mod r): p=5 seeds 1 and 2 (ξ=2, 1) and p=7 (ξ=3) recovered; p=11 and p=13 runs in progress. Step 3: min-fill widths d=3..10 = 17, 25, 35, 41, 44, 53, 63, 62; fit 6.69·d−0.99; contraction time tracks 2^width (0.09 s → 11.2 s for width 18 → 25); extrapolated width at d=131 ≈ 876 (2^876 entries per contraction) against Pollard rho ≈ 2^65.3 group operations for ECCp-131.

### Decisions made
Exact contraction stands in for the unreviewed OpenAI algorithm; results measure this contractor, not the paper's bound. Local CPU only; no RunPod or cloud launch (the RunPod key is not visible to this session, and no launch was authorised for this work). End-to-end solves limited to d ≤ 4: width 35 at d=5 exceeds 15 GB.

### Open threads
p=11 and p=13 end-to-end records (runs/ic_p11_s1, ic_p13_s1). The paper's O(d) multiplier is not built; whether it lowers width is untested.

### Errors
Coordinates not range-checked below p gave overcounts (22 vs 6); fixed with range checks. Duplicate variables in a predicate's scope (squaring) gave fractional counts after pinning (1.5); fixed in `Net.add`. `matplotlib` was missing; installed with pip.

## 2026-10-30T04:03:34Z — hashed-Boolean arm in the ic pipeline

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- Added `src/cryptanalysis/ic_framework/hashed.rs`: `hashed-affine`
  (`FactorBaseBuilder<PrimeCurve>`, union of `m` affine-hashed slot bases) and
  `hashed-tensor` (`DecompositionOracle<PrimeCurve>`, exact Boolean
  tensor-contraction witness from `crates/hashed-ecdl-tensor`).
- Registered both in `src/bin/ic/bench.rs` for the prime regime and in
  `ic bench --list`; documented the arm in `docs/ic/FRAMEWORK.md` §9.
- Added `tests/hashed_ic_pipeline.rs` and `examples/hashed_ic_smoke.rs`.
- `cargo run --release --example hashed_ic_smoke`: base 12 points/12 columns,
  2-slot witness replays to the target, 0 integrity failures, 62,922 charged
  contraction steps.
- `target/release/ic bench --bits 7 --factor-base hashed-affine:slots=2
  --oracle hashed-tensor:slots=2 --max-trials 200 --repeats 1 --rho-runs 0`:
  13 relations, 13 rows, rank 13, verified (`all_verified: true`), 35.5 s.
- Toolchain: built with rustup 1.99.1 (the Homebrew rustc 1.93.1 cannot
  satisfy the AWS crates' 1.94.1 floor).

### Decisions made (and why)

- The algorithm is exposed as the two stages it actually is, not as a fork of
  the pipeline: one uniform base with one column per point (no negation fold),
  and per-slot affine hashes. The oracle rebuilds the same slots in `prepare`
  and refuses the run if the point list differs.
- Contraction work is charged as `hashed_tensor_steps`, an uncalibrated
  primitive count. Because no calibration ratio exists yet, a row's `S`
  does not price it; the docs mark the arm a stage diagnostic, not a
  comparable end-to-end `S`.
- `slots`, `seed`, `tau` and `s` must match between the factor base and the
  oracle; defaults are 2, 1, the Lemma 13 ceiling and the first prime in
  `[2p,4p]`.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

- No commit or PR yet (worktree changes only).
- The contraction step needs a calibration before this arm's `S` can be read
  as an end-to-end cost.
- The arm is prime-field only; binary/Koblitz hashed bases are not wired.
- No 10-bit run completed inside 300 s; the 7-bit run is the working smoke.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

## 2026-10-30T04:31:00Z — hashed-arm accounting and non-roster sizes

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- `crates/hashed-ecdl-tensor/src/lib.rs`: `Meter` now records
  `curve_additions` and `square_roots` from the existing phase labels, with no
  extra map or per-step allocation.
- `src/cryptanalysis/ic_framework/hashed.rs`: enumeration charges curve
  additions to `GroupOps` and square roots to `sqrt_solves`; `prepare` does the
  same; `decompose` charges the contraction's curve additions and Tonelli-Shanks
  calls. `hashed_tensor_steps` remains the diagnostic primitive count.
- `src/cryptanalysis/ic_framework/mod.rs`: a `hashed-tensor` row now carries
  `partial_counted_cost: tensor-contraction primitive steps are uncalibrated,
  so S excludes most of the relation phase`, which also suppresses `s_over_rho`.
- `src/bin/ic/bench.rs`: `--bits` falls back to `find_prime_order_curve` for
  8..=32 when the roster has no curve, so the arm can be tested at 8, 9, 10 …
- `examples/hashed_ic_smoke.rs` and `tests/hashed_ic_pipeline.rs` formatted;
  smoke run: 12 points/12 columns, 2-slot witness replays, 62,912 steps.

### Decisions made (and why)

- Curve additions from the tensor backend are now real `GroupOps`; square roots
  are a calibrated native unit. Boolean edge-assignment steps are not group
  operations and have no ratio, so they stay uncalibrated and the row is
  explicitly qualified rather than given a fabricated `S`.
- Kept the primitive-step counter for diagnostics; it is the only measure of the
  contraction's Boolean work until a calibration exists.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

- The arm is verified at 7 bits (13 relations, rank 13) and runs at 8 bits; it
  is exponential, so 10-bit did not finish inside 300 s.
- `ca-ic` lives in `cryptanalysis/suite`, which does not vendor
  `hashed-ecdl-tensor`; a separate port is needed there.
- No commit or PR.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

- `--bits 8` with 5 trials ran 49 s and did not verify; with a larger trial
  budget it needs minutes. Recorded as a size limit, not a correctness failure.

### 23:40 UTC — Rust port of the ecdltensor Go prototype (start)

#### What was done (commands, files, features)

Fetched carrier branch `research/ecdl-tensor-go-source-20261029` (commit
1c29f66, standalone Go module `ecdltensor`, 3,364 lines incl. tests and two
commands) into a scratch directory. Reference run: `go test ./...` passed in
43.2 s (go 1.24.7 (/usr/local/go)); `go run ./cmd/ecdlscale` reproduced the README table
(mul e/b 35.2, 31.4, 29.1, 27.7, 27.0; max degree 352, 1248, 2304, 8768, 17024
for b = 64…16384). Conductor task T-1 created and claimed (`conductor check`:
clear). Branch `claude/ecdl-tensor-ic-rust-port-20261029`. Launched Workflow
`port-ecdltensor-to-rust` (run wf_bb43169a-ab3): Sonnet skeleton agent, six
parallel Sonnet port agents, Sonnet build/fix loop, independent verifiers.

#### Decisions made (and why)

New crate `crates/ecdl-tensor-ic`, standalone with its own Cargo.lock and CI
workflow, mirroring `crates/hashed-ecdl-tensor` (the root has no Cargo
workspace; crates are path packages). Big integers via `num-bigint` 0.4, the
root package's dependency. Not added as a root dependency, so the root build
and lockfile are untouched. This is a research prototype port, not a
performance result: no leaderboard, benchmarks/ or validation-gate rows, and
no resume checkpoint (no experiment run). Go's math/rand streams are not
reproduced bit-for-bit; deterministic structure (edge counts, degrees) must
match exactly.

#### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Workflow result, independent check of tests and scale table, report/graph/PDF,
PR with auto-merge, PR follow-up.

#### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

cairn, conductor-mcp, isolab and isolab-eks MCP servers failed to connect;
the `conductor` CLI works and was used instead. None of the others is needed
for a code port.
